can you explain it for this guy

**A:**

→ Dark pools are private exchanges where institutional investors trade large blocks of securities away from public order books, the primary goal is to execute massive orders without triggering immediate price impact or signaling intent to the wider market.

→ Transactions are processed by the dark pool operator (usually a large bank or broker-dealer like Goldman Sachs or Morgan Stanley) using internal matching engines, they match buy and sell orders internally before ever touching a public exchange.

→ You’re right on the directionality: a large print in a dark pool is just a record of a completed trade, not a live order. It’s often a institutional rebalance or a hedge, and because the trade is already done, it doesn't carry the same "buy/sell pressure" signal as a public limit order.

→ If you see a massive print, don't assume it's a directional bet, look for the volume relative to the average daily volume (ADV) to gauge institutional interest, but remember that the "why" behind the trade is hidden until the 13F filings drop months later.

## 2026-06-02 01:19:40 UTC

**Asker:** BK (`bankerkyle`) in #💬-stonks-yapping-💬

**Q:** what are the stocks that will benefit the most from Trump cutting agricultural equipment tariffs by 15-25%?

**A:**

→ The agricultural equipment sector, led by **$DE**, **$AGCO**, and **$CNH**, has been squeezed by higher input costs (steel/aluminum) and regulatory compliance burdens, not just direct equipment tariffs.

→ Relief here is driven by two levers: potential easing of environmental regulations on machinery, which the administration has explicitly targeted to lower manufacturing costs, and any reduction in raw material import duties.

→ **$DE** (John Deere) is the primary beneficiary of margin expansion if input costs drop, given its massive domestic manufacturing footprint, though it remains heavily tied to farmer sentiment and crop prices (soybeans/corn), which are currently depressed.

→ **$AGCO** is more exposed to tariff volatility due to its higher reliance on European-sourced components, making it a higher-beta play on trade policy shifts.

→ The real bottleneck isn't just the cost of the tractor, but the farmer’s ability to pay, until crop prices recover, margin relief for manufacturers may be offset by continued weak demand for large-large scale equipment upgrades.

[YOU said earlier]: → Dark pools are private exchanges where institutional investors trade large blocks of securities away from public order books, the primary goal is to execute massive orders without triggering immediate price impact or signaling intent to the wider market.

→ Transactions are processed by the dark pool operator (usually a large bank or broker-dealer like Goldman Sachs or Morgan Stanley) using internal matching engines, they match buy and sell orders internally before ever touching a public exchange.

→ Transactions are processed by the dark pool operator (usually a large bank or broker-dealer like Goldman Sachs or Morgan Stanley) using internal matching engines, they match buy and sell orders internally before ever touching a public exchange.
